---

### GLOB-01: Wire contracts are transport-specific
* **The Decision:** Transport protocols define their own physical encoding (CAN frames, Modbus registers, BLE GATT, HTTP REST, MQTT JSON) rather than forcing an identical byte packet across all media.
* **The Rule:** Adapters deserialize native transport packets into canonical internal command structures. Internal business logic has zero transport awareness.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** A mobile app connects over BLE and issues a target update via an 8-byte characteristic. An automation system sends the same update via HTTP JSON. Both deserialize into an identical canonical command structure with zero business-logic differences.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** An external integration sends malformed Modbus frames. The Modbus adapter rejects the packet at the transport boundary. Core control loops and other network adapters (HTTP/BLE) continue running with zero memory corruption or CPU starvation.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-02: Timestamp encoding is transport-specific
* **The Decision:** Time representations on the wire are optimized for the transport medium (compact millisecond offsets on CAN, ISO-8601 strings on HTTP).
* **The Rule:** Transport adapters convert wire timestamps into canonical 64-bit microsecond representations upon ingestion.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** A high-throughput CAN bus uses compact 16-bit millisecond offsets, keeping bus utilization low while maintaining temporal accuracy.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** A remote client sends an HTTP packet with an unparseable timestamp format. The HTTP adapter flags the timestamp as invalid and assigns local receive time with an `UNTRUSTED_TIMESTAMP` quality tag, preventing bad time data from corrupting the scheduler.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-03: Request vs. result vs. state distinction
* **The Decision:** The ecosystem strictly separates a command request, the processing outcome, and the resulting runtime state.
* **The Rule:** Receiving an instruction does not imply success, and returning an execution status does not represent live hardware feedback.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** A valve open command returns `APPLIED`. The valve driver engages. Once the limit switch closes, the runtime state transitions to `OPEN`.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** A pump start command is received. The Command Manager validates the request and returns `ACCEPTED`. Before the contactor engages, an overcurrent sensor trips. The command outcome records `FAILED`, and the runtime state remains `STOPPED`. Callers querying state see that the pump never actually ran.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-04: Global timeout & deadline framework
* **The Decision:** All bounded operations define explicit activation conditions, cancellation triggers, and timeout failure behaviors.
* **The Rule:** Operations must specify monotonic timeout deadlines. Open-ended wait loops (`while(!done)`) are strictly prohibited.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** A fill valve is commanded open with a 60-second fill deadline. Fluid reaches the target level at second 35; the level switch cancels the timeout, and the valve closes cleanly.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** A tank level sensor wire breaks while filling. Fluid never registers. At second 60, the monotonic deadline fires, forcing the valve closed and latching a `FILL_TIMEOUT_FAULT`, preventing an overflow.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-05: Fault recovery rules reside in the Operational Matrix
* **The Decision:** Post-fault operational eligibility is evaluated by the Operational Matrix, not inferred by fault monitors.
* **The Rule:** When a fault condition clears, the Fault Engine updates health records, but the Operational Matrix determines whether, when, and how actuation may resume.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** A transient motor stall fault clears. The Operational Matrix checks system state, confirms all safety interlocks are satisfied, and permits the control loop to restart the motor.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** A technician repairs a thermal sensor, clearing an over-temperature fault. However, an independent duct pressure interlock remains open. The OM evaluates current conditions and continues blocking fan restart, preventing startup under partial fault conditions.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-06: Universal communication loss framework
* **The Decision:** Communication loss uses standardized ping-freshness thresholds and recovery windows across all nodes.
* **The Rule:** Loss of peer communication transitions the relationship to `DISCONNECTED`. Autonomous local control continues; dependent inter-node coordination sessions terminate safely.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** Heartbeat pings arrive every 5 seconds. The connection remains healthy, and distributed coordination runs smoothly.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** A Wi-Fi router reboots during an inter-node purge cycle. The heartbeat timer exceeds its 30-second threshold. The node enters `DISCONNECTED` state, aborts the shared purge routine, and reverts to its autonomous local baseline.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-07: Normalized resource quality semantics
* **The Decision:** Control loops evaluate normalized quality states (`GOOD`, `STALE`, `INVALID`, `UNAVAILABLE`) rather than low-level bus errors.
* **The Rule:** Sensor drivers translate physical anomalies (I2C NACK, ADC rail saturation, checksum error) into standard quality enums.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** An I2C temperature probe reads cleanly. The Resource Engine tags the value `GOOD`. The climate loop uses the reading directly.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** A sensor cable shorts to ground, pulling the ADC input to rail maximum. The hardware driver detects out-of-range voltage and tags the reading `INVALID`. The control loop sees `INVALID` and transitions to degraded mode rather than interpreting the reading as an extreme temperature.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-08: Structured fault lifecycle
* **The Decision:** Faults are managed as structured state machines (detecting, active, latched, clearing, recovered), not informal boolean flags.
* **The Rule:** Fault transitions carry timestamps, debounce counters, severity ratings, and recovery rules.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** An optical sensor detects a single transient dust particle. The debounce counter increments to 1 of 3, then resets on the next clean sample. No fault is raised.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** Dust permanently blocks the optical sensor. The debounce counter increments through 3 consecutive evaluation windows. The Fault Engine transitions the condition to `ACTIVE`, logs a structured error with timestamp, and alerts the monitoring system.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-09: Events represent transitions, not continuous state
* **The Decision:** Events announce discrete state transitions; ongoing conditions are represented as state.
* **The Rule:** Event bus emissions fire only when a state variable changes value or an operational threshold is crossed.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** A fan accelerates from $0\%$ to $50\%$. A single `FAN_STARTED` event fires. While running steadily at $50\%$, zero events are emitted, keeping network traffic low.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** A noisy sensor fluctuates around a threshold. The driver applies hysteresis; events fire only when the value crosses the filtered boundaries, preventing event bus saturation.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-10: Single canonical inbound command pipeline
* **The Decision:** All commands pass through an identical validation, safety gating, and dispatch pipeline regardless of transport source.
* **The Rule:** Adapters deserialize commands and feed them into the Command Manager. Direct calls to hardware drivers from network handlers are prohibited.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** A setpoint command sent over BLE passes through the same range validation and safety checks as an identical command sent via Modbus.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** A malicious or buggy script sends an out-of-range fan speed ($250\%$) via HTTP REST. The Command Manager intercepts the packet, rejects it at the validation gate, and returns an error without the command reaching the actuator driver.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-11: Deterministic authority resolution
* **The Decision:** Conflicting commands are resolved by explicit authority precedence, never by network arrival order or callback timing.
* **The Rule:** Actuator authority follows fixed priority:
  $$\text{SAFETY} > \text{CALIBRATION} > \text{COMPLIANCE} > \text{MANUAL} > \text{AUTO} > \text{DEFAULT/OFF}$$
* **Best-Case Scenario:** An automation loop runs in `AUTO`. A user presses a local manual override button. Authority transfers to `MANUAL`, executing the user command.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** An automation script commands a valve open at the exact millisecond a high-level safety sensor trips. The Operational Matrix evaluates Safety priority above Automation, forcing the valve closed regardless of packet timing.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-12: Explicit request supersession semantics
* **The Decision:** When a new command arrives, existing active commands are explicitly updated or cancelled according to defined supersession rules.
* **The Rule:** A newly accepted target supersedes an in-flight transition. The driver re-routes toward the newest target without completing obsolete transitions.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** Fan is ramping from $20\%$ to $80\%$. At $40\%$, a new command arrives requesting $50\%$. The ramp profiler updates destination to $50\%$ and decelerates cleanly.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** Multiple automations fight over setpoints, sending conflicting targets. The Command Manager applies supersession, executing the latest validated command without buffering obsolete commands.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-13: Capability mask separated from runtime health
* **The Decision:** Disabling a hardware feature changes its configuration capability mask; it does not clear existing health records or fault history.
* **The Rule:** Configuration capability defines whether a feature is provisioned. Runtime health tracks physical hardware state.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** A node is deployed without an optional current sensor. The configuration mask flags current sensing as `DISABLED`. Control loops bypass current validation checks.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** An operator disables a failing sensor in software to silence an alarm. The capability mask updates to `DISABLED`, bypassing the interlock, but the historical fault log remains intact in flash for diagnostic auditing.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-14: Four cardinal separations
* **The Decision:** The ecosystem enforces strict separation between Configuration, Runtime State, Commands, and Events.
* **The Rule:** Firmware layers must not conflate these categories. Runtime state never auto-persists to flash; commands never act as state variables.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** Device reboots. It reads configuration setpoints from flash, recomputes runtime state fresh from sensors, and enters normal operation cleanly.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** A system crash occurs during an active ramp. On reboot, the device loads the configured target setpoint from flash rather than an intermediate volatile ramp position, preventing state corruption.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-15: Distributed shared-setpoint synchronization
* **The Decision:** Nodes sharing setpoints synchronize using monotonic versions and timestamps; nodes retain local authority to accept or reject updates.
* **The Rule:** Incoming setpoint synchronization packets must carry a newer timestamp than the locally stored value to be applied.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** A user updates target temperature on the Thermostat. The Thermostat broadcasts the update with a new timestamp. The EQ receives the packet, validates recency, and updates its local setpoint.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** An older, delayed network packet arrives after a user has made a local adjustment. The node compares timestamps, sees the incoming packet is older than its local setting, and drops the stale update.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-16: Stable global network & node identity model
* **The Decision:** Every node advertises a stable identity (`node_type`, `node_instance_id`, `firmware_version`, `protocol_version`).
* **The Rule:** Messages and telemetry packets carry source node identifiers.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** The Hub scans the network, identifies `EQ-001`, `THERMOSTAT-001`, and `TANK-001`, and populates dashboard widgets accordingly.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** Two identical nodes are flashed with the same factory image. On first boot, the network manager detects an address collision, transitions the duplicate node to an unconfigured state, and prompts the operator to assign a unique instance ID.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-17: Independent contract versioning
* **The Decision:** Protocol versions, configuration schemas, and firmware versions are versioned independently.
* **The Rule:** A firmware update that does not alter communication contracts must not increment the protocol version.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** Node firmware is updated to optimize an internal motor driver. Protocol version remains unchanged; the node communicates with the existing Hub without requiring gateway updates.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** A major protocol upgrade adds new telemetry fields. Older nodes continue communicating using protocol version 1, while updated nodes use version 2, maintaining backward compatibility.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-18: Dynamic capability discovery across nodes
* **The Decision:** Nodes discover peer capabilities via dynamic capability masks rather than assuming identical hardware builds.
* **The Rule:** Querying a node returns its active capability bitmask. Callers must verify capability support before commanding optional features.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** The Hub queries `TANK-001`, detects pump control capability, and renders pump toggle switches on the dashboard.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** A dashboard sends a flap position command to an EQ configured without a motorized damper. The EQ evaluates its capability mask, sees flap control is unprovisioned, and returns `REJECTED [REASON: CAPABILITY_UNAVAILABLE]`.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-19: Normalized resource health vocabulary
* **The Decision:** System health is expressed using standard states (`GOOD`, `DEGRADED`, `FAULTED`, `UNAVAILABLE`) across all devices.
* **The Rule:** Diagnostics engines normalize subsystem health into standard enums for system-wide reporting.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** All devices report health as `GOOD`. The master dashboard displays a green status icon across the entire installation.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** A backup sensor fails on `EQ-002`. It reports health as `DEGRADED`. The master dashboard flags the node for maintenance while autonomous climate control continues running.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-20: Operational Matrix is a static declarative guardrail
* **The Decision:** The Operational Matrix is a static compile-time rulebook, not a dynamic runtime controller.
* **The Rule:** Safety tables are defined in firmware. Users and network interfaces cannot modify safety interlock logic at runtime.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** Control loops submit requests to the OM. The OM evaluates compiled rules and authorizes safe actuation.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** An operator attempts to write a configuration register to bypass a low-water pump interlock. The Command Manager rejects the write; safety equations are hard-coded in firmware and cannot be disabled via configuration.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-21: Safety is highest actuator authority
* **The Decision:** Safety interlocks and emergency-stop mechanisms override all user, auto, and compliance commands.
* **The Rule:** When a safety interlock trips, actuator drivers execute an immediate safe shutdown, regardless of active operating mode.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** All safety interlocks are clear. Motors, valves, and heaters respond smoothly to user and automation commands.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** An automation loop commands full ventilation while an emergency stop button is pressed. The safety interlock trips, overriding the automation command and driving the motor to an immediate halt.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-22: Network participant lifecycle states
* **The Decision:** Node network presence follows explicit states: `DISCOVERED`, `INITIALIZED`, `VALIDATED`, `ONLINE`, `DEGRADED`, `OFFLINE`.
* **The Rule:** Nodes must pass through validation and handshake phases before being admitted to active coordination.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** A new node powers on, broadcasts discovery, exchanges capability masks with the Hub, validates encryption keys, and transitions to `ONLINE`.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** A node with corrupted calibration attempts to connect. The Hub validates identity, detects a calibration failure, and moves the node to `DEGRADED`, isolating it from active coordination while alerting technicians.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-23: Reconnection state reconciliation
* **The Decision:** Reconnecting nodes synchronize state using full snapshots rather than replaying missed historical commands.
* **The Rule:** After a communication dropout, nodes exchange current state snapshots and discard stale intermediate command queues.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** A brief network glitch drops connection for 5 seconds. Reconnection triggers a snapshot sync; nodes verify they are in sync and resume normal updates.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** A node is offline for 2 hours while a technician adjusts setpoints locally. Upon reconnecting, the node does not replay 2 hours of buffered network commands; it publishes its current state snapshot, establishing local truth.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-24: Recency/timestamp handling for out-of-order packets
* **The Decision:** Distributed messages carry timestamps; older updates arriving out of order are dropped.
* **The Rule:** Network receivers compare incoming packet timestamps against the last processed timestamp, discarding older arrivals.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** Packets arrive in sequence. Each update advances the internal state timestamp smoothly.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** Network jitter causes Packet 1 (sent at t=0) to arrive after Packet 2 (sent at t=1). The receiver processes Packet 2, then inspects Packet 1, detects its older timestamp, and drops it, preventing state reversion.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-25: Machine-readable diagnostic reason codes
* **The Decision:** Rejections, warnings, and state transitions publish standardized, machine-readable reason codes alongside human-readable logs.
* **The Rule:** Error structures must include a numeric or enum reason code (`REASON_INTERLOCK_BLOCKED`, `REASON_SENSOR_TIMEOUT`).
* **Best-Case Scenario:** A command succeeds, returning `STATUS_APPLIED` with `REASON_SUCCESS`. The UI updates without parsing text strings.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** An automated test suite injects faults. Instead of parsing localized log text, the test harness asserts on numeric reason codes (`0x42: ERR_STALL`), enabling automated verification.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-26: Observed Health $\neq$ Active Fault $\neq$ Retained Latch
* **The Decision:** System monitoring tracks Observed Health, Active Faults, and Retained Latches as independent dimensions.
* **The Rule:** A healthy physical reading clears an ordinary fault, but never clears a retained safety latch.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** A sensor reading drops momentarily. The signal normalizes, passes three consecutive evaluations, and the active fault flag clears automatically.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** Severe motor over-current trips a latching safety fault. Power is cut. Sensor readings return to zero current (healthy), but the latch remains active in NVS, preventing motor restart until a technician issues a verified clear command.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-27: Fault Engine does not own actuation or permissions
* **The Decision:** The Fault Engine records conditions; the Operational Matrix decides permission; physical drivers execute actuation.
* **The Rule:** Fault monitors must not call actuator drivers directly. All operational consequences flow through the Operational Matrix.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** A sensor fails. The Fault Engine flags the fault. The control loop queries the Operational Matrix, which returns `DEGRADED`. The control loop adjusts its target accordingly.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** A bug in a fault monitor raises an erroneous alarm. Because the monitor cannot directly manipulate hardware, the Operational Matrix evaluates surrounding conditions, confirms backup sensors are valid, and keeps the system running safely.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-28: Operational permissions evaluate current conditions
* **The Decision:** The Operational Matrix evaluates current operating facts, not historical fault causes.
* **The Rule:** Operational decisions are based on the live system state at the moment of evaluation, ensuring identical logic during boot, runtime, and recovery.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** A startup check evaluates current sensor health, interlock states, and configuration, authorizing clean system activation.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** A node recovers from a transient brownout. The OM evaluates current voltages and safety lines rather than historical brownout flags, allowing the system to resume operation without an unnecessary hard reset.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-29: Operational Matrix is the safety policy evaluator
* **The Decision:** Safety rules are expressed and evaluated directly within the Operational Matrix.
* **The Rule:** Safety gating occurs inside the OM table. Separate, parallel safety controllers are prohibited.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** A valve open command arrives. The OM evaluates line pressure, tank capacity, and safety switches in a single pass, returning `PERMITTED`.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** An engineer adds a new high-pressure safety switch. Instead of writing custom safety code across multiple controllers, the switch is added as an input predicate to the Valve OM, protecting all actuation paths.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-30: Centralized Fault Engine owns health integration
* **The Decision:** Where a node includes a Fault Engine, it acts as the single authority for integrated health tracking.
* **The Rule:** Subsystems report health evidence to the Fault Engine; individual modules do not maintain private health registries.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** Sensor, driver, and communication monitors report to the Fault Engine. Telemetry queries read a unified health status from a single source.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** A communication driver suffers packet drops while a sensor bus experiences read errors. Both report to the Fault Engine, which correlates the timing, identifies a shared power rail issue, and logs a unified diagnostic.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-31: Dependency-based aggregate health
* **The Decision:** Node health is derived from capability dependencies; optional sensor failures degrade the node without marking it fully unavailable.
* **The Rule:** Failure of a required dependency marks the capability `UNAVAILABLE`. Failure of an optional dependency marks the node `DEGRADED`.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** An optional ambient humidity sensor fails on an EQ configured for temperature balancing. The node reports `DEGRADED`, but temperature regulation continues unaffected.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** The primary level transducer fails on a Tank node with no backup. Because the sensor is a required dependency, the node marks fluid management `UNAVAILABLE`, safely halting the transfer pump.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-32: Resource Engine owns logical ingestion & resolution
* **The Decision:** The Resource Engine manages all sensor ingestion, normalization, and provider selection.
* **The Rule:** Control loops consume logical resources (`INTERNAL_TEMPERATURE`), remaining completely decoupled from physical sensor buses.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** A control loop reads `AMBIENT_TEMP`. The Resource Engine handles bus polling, checksum validation, and unit scaling, delivering a clean Kelvin float.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** A hardware revision replaces an I2C temperature sensor with a Modbus probe. The driver layer is updated, but the climate control loop remains untouched, reading the same logical resource.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-33: Provider eligibility precedes arbitration
* **The Decision:** Providers must pass validity, quality, and fault checks before participating in arbitration.
* **The Rule:** Ineligible candidates (corrupted CRC, stale beyond limits, provider in fault) are filtered out before arbitration runs.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** Two providers are online. Both pass eligibility checks. Freshness-first arbitration selects the provider with the newest sample.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** A primary sensor begins outputting corrupted packets with current timestamps. The driver fails CRC validation, marking the provider `INVALID`. The eligibility gate drops it, and arbitration selects the older, valid backup sensor.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-34: Temporal provenance: source time vs. receive time
* **The Decision:** Sensor readings record both the source measurement timestamp and the local receive timestamp.
* **The Rule:** Measurement age is calculated from source time; local receive time is used to monitor network latency.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** A wireless sensor packet arrives with a 20ms delta between source and receive time, verifying fresh data and low latency.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** A mesh network stalls, buffering packets for 3 minutes before delivery. Local receive time is current, but source timestamps reveal the data is stale, preventing the node from using outdated readings.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-35: Derived resources are composable with cycle rejection
* **The Decision:** Derived calculations (`AVG`, `MAX`, `DELTA`) can consume other resources, but dependency cycles ($A \to B \to A$) are rejected at configuration time.
* **The Rule:** The configuration parser builds a directed acyclic graph (DAG) of resource dependencies, rejecting any circular references during validation.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** Four tank level sensors feed into an `AVG` derived resource, providing an accurate average volume metric.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** An operator accidentally configures Resource A to derive from Resource B, which derives from Resource A. The configuration validator detects the cycle, rejects the transaction, and keeps the previous valid configuration active.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-36: Component-local Operational Matrices
* **The Decision:** Each physical component or subsystem owns an independent Operational Matrix.
* **The Rule:** Component matrices are isolated. One component's OM cannot directly invoke or override another component's OM.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** EQ Fan evaluates the Fan OM before running. Flap state is passed as a boolean predicate.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** An update to Tank valve safety rules requires modifying the Valve OM. Because matrices are isolated, the Tank pump matrix, EQ matrices, and Thermostat matrices remain unchanged.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-37: Conjunctive AND evaluation for OM predicates
* **The Decision:** Operational Matrix predicates are evaluated as conjunctive AND equations; all required predicates must pass for an operation to be permitted.
* **The Rule:** Every required condition in the predicate chain must evaluate to true:
  $$\text{Permission} = P_1 \land P_2 \land P_3 \dots \land P_n$$
* **Best-Case Scenario:** A fan start request evaluates E-stop clear, lockout off, flap open, and sensors valid. All predicates pass; the OM returns `PERMITTED`.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** A fan start request evaluates all operating conditions as valid, but the flap interlock evaluates to false. The predicate chain fails; the OM returns `PROHIBITED [REASON: FLAP_NOT_OPEN]`.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-38: Standardized OM vocabulary
* **The Decision:** Standardizes operational outcomes to `PERMITTED`, `DEGRADED`, `UNAVAILABLE`, and `PROHIBITED`.
* **The Rule:** Subsystems use standardized outcome enums for all operational permission decisions.
* **Status:** SPECIALIZED by `GLOB-40` (which removed `FALLBACK`).

### GLOB-39: Universal severity hierarchy for OM outcomes
* **The Decision:** Evaluates multiple conditions using a fixed severity hierarchy:
  $$\text{PERMITTED} < \text{DEGRADED} < \text{UNAVAILABLE} < \text{PROHIBITED}$$
* **The Rule:** When multiple conditions evaluate simultaneously, the OM returns the single most restrictive outcome based on the severity hierarchy.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** An optional display is disconnected (`DEGRADED`), while sensor inputs are healthy. The OM returns `DEGRADED`, allowing the node to operate.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** An optional sensor is disconnected (`DEGRADED`), while an emergency stop line trips (`PROHIBITED`). The OM resolves to `PROHIBITED`, shutting down all actuators.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-40: `FALLBACK` removed from OM vocabulary
* **The Decision:** Provider fallback is an internal Resource Engine operation, not an Operational Matrix outcome.
* **The Rule:** The OM evaluates whether the resulting logical resource is sufficient for the requested operation; it does not track provider switching mechanics.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** Primary temperature sensor drops out; Resource Engine switches to backup. The backup provides valid data, so the OM returns `PERMITTED`.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** Both primary and backup temperature sensors fail. The Resource Engine marks the logical resource `UNAVAILABLE`. The OM evaluates the missing resource and returns `UNAVAILABLE`, safely suspending operation.
* **Status:** LOCKED (Supersedes `FALLBACK` in `GLOB-38`).

### GLOB-41: User input controls must use the command pipeline
* **The Decision:** Physical inputs (buttons, rotary encoders) issue semantic commands to the Command Manager; they never inject raw values into the Resource Engine.
* **The Rule:** User interactions generate command packets that pass through standard validation, logging, and safety gating.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** An operator turns a dial. The UI adapter sends a command to increase fan speed. The command is validated, approved by the OM, and applied through the Fan Driver.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** An electrical glitch causes a push-button to chatter rapidly while a device is locked out. The raw inputs generate commands that are rejected at the validation gate, protecting the control loop from erratic behavior.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-42: Invalid data must not reset age of retained data
* **The Decision:** Receiving an invalid reading preserves the last valid reading up to its expiration limit, but does not reset its measurement age.
* **The Rule:** Retained values age continuously from their original measurement timestamp. An invalid packet cannot refresh data freshness.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** A sensor sampled every second drops one packet. The previous reading is retained for that second, keeping the control loop steady.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** A probe cable is damaged, generating continuous bus noise. The Resource Engine retains the last valid reading. Because invalid packets do not reset the age counter, the reading ages normally and flags as `EXPIRED` after 10 seconds, safely halting dependent outputs.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-43: Three operational stages: fresh, stale, expired
* **The Decision:** Resource freshness is explicitly classified as **Fresh** (normal operation), **Stale** (degraded, fallback allowed), or **Expired** (unusable, resource unavailable).
* **The Rule:** Data within the freshness window is `FRESH`. Data exceeding the window but within the retention limit is `STALE`. Data exceeding the retention limit is `EXPIRED`.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** Sensor packets arrive every 2 seconds against a 5-second freshness window. Readings remain continuously tagged as `FRESH`.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** A wireless sensor experiences transmission interference. At second 6, the reading transitions to `STALE`; control loops enter degraded mode. At second 60, the reading transitions to `EXPIRED`; control loops safely suspend operation.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-44: Receipt time does not prove observation freshness
* **The Decision:** A newly received network packet carrying an old reading is marked stale; local receipt time does not establish measurement freshness.
* **The Rule:** Freshness calculations compare source measurement timestamps against current time.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** A network packet arrives with minimal transport delay. Source time confirms data freshness; local time verifies low network latency.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** A network router buffers packets during a reconnect, delivering a batch of 5-minute-old sensor packets. Local receipt time is current, but source timestamps reveal the data is stale, preventing the node from acting on outdated information.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-45: Global freshness and staleness limits by default
* **The Decision:** Freshness, stale, and expiration thresholds follow ecosystem-wide defaults; resource-specific exceptions require explicit architectural justification.
* **The Rule:** Subsystems inherit standard global timing limits unless an approved node specification overrides them.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** A new sensor type is added to the system. It inherits standard freshness and expiration limits automatically, functioning predictably without custom timing code.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** A slow-response chemical sensor requires a longer sampling window. An explicit configuration override updates its limits, while all standard sensors continue using ecosystem defaults.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

### GLOB-46: Kelvin is the canonical internal temperature unit
* **The Decision:** All internal temperature storage, calculations, resource engines, and setpoint comparisons use **Kelvin**; conversion to Celsius or Fahrenheit occurs strictly at I/O boundaries.
* **The Rule:** Normalization occurs at ingestion. Presentation scaling (Celsius, Fahrenheit) occurs strictly at display boundaries.
* **Best-Case Scenario:** An imperial probe reports $68^\circ\text{F}$ and a metric probe reports $20^\circ\text{C}$. Both normalize to $293.15\text{ K}$, allowing direct mathematical comparison.
* **Worst-Case Scenario:** Legacy code mixed Celsius setpoints with Fahrenheit sensor inputs, causing mathematical errors that locked heating loops. Canonical Kelvin math eliminates mixed-unit calculation errors across all nodes.
* **Status:** LOCKED.

---

# 11. Appendage Landing Protocol & Change Control

To preserve document integrity during future development, all updates must follow this protocol:

1. **Strict Append-Only Rule:** New architectural decisions are appended to the document using the next sequential identifier (`GLOB-47`, `GLOB-48`, etc.).
2. **Never Renumber Existing IDs:** Established `GLOB-XX` identifiers are permanent references. They are never renumbered or reordered.
3. **Explicit Supersession:** If a new decision modifies an existing rule, the old decision remains in the register marked **SUPERSEDED BY GLOB-YY**, and the new decision records **SUPERSEDES GLOB-XX**.
4. **Promotion from Node Documents:** When an EQ, Thermostat, or Tanks design discussion produces an engine or rule relevant to other nodes, it is assigned the next sequential `GLOB-XX` ID and added here. The node document then references that Global ID.
5. **No Code Implementations in Global:** Wire-level packet definitions, C++ struct memory layouts, and GPIO mappings belong in their respective stage work packages, not in this architectural specification.

FILE: E:\GARUDA\git\garuda-controls\sentinel\specs\nodework.md, encoding=UTF-8
To build this system from the ground up, we take a **bottom-up, dependency-driven engineering approach**. 

You cannot write a climate control loop if you don't have normalized sensor inputs; you cannot feed targets to a fan if the physical PWM driver and safety interlocks don't exist; and you cannot save setpoints if flash persistence isn't operational.

Here is the complete architectural implementation blueprint: the directory structure, the file responsibilities, and the exact step-by-step order to build everything from bare silicon to the autonomous Equalizer.

---

# Part 1: Repository & Directory Architecture

We cleanly decouple **Universal Reusable Code** (`global/`) from **Node-Specific Logic** (`nodes/eq/`).

```text
garuda_firmware/
│
├── global/                                 <-- REUSABLE ACROSS ALL NODES (EQ, Thermostat, Tanks, Hub)
│   ├── core/
│   │   ├── garuda_types.h                  <-- Universal enums, quality tags, units, outcome enums
│   │   ├── monotonic_clock.h / .cpp        <-- Non-stepping tick timers & watchdog duration trackers
│   │   ├── resource_engine.h / .cpp        <-- Logical sensor ingestion, freshness, Kelvin math, arbitration
│   │   ├── operational_matrix.h            <-- Base declarative AND-evaluator & severity resolver template
│   │   ├── fault_engine.h / .cpp           <-- Health vs. Fault vs. Latch, debounce, POST reconciliation
│   │   ├── command_manager.h / .cpp        <-- Canonical command gateway, validation, outcome routing
│   │   └── persistence_manager.h / .cpp    <-- NVS flash wrapper, CRC32 verification, debounced commits
│   │
│   ├── drivers/                            <-- PURE HARDWARE DRIVERS (Zero business logic / policy)
│   │   ├── fan_driver.h / .cpp             <-- PWM generation, relay GPIO, startup boost, tach RPM/current
│   │   ├── servo_driver.h / .cpp           <-- Pulse-width mapping, inversion, slew rate stepping, coil detach
│   │   └── relay_driver.h / .cpp           <-- Generic isolated relay output driver (reusable for pumps/HVAC)
│   │
│   ├── comms/
│   │   └── comms_contracts.h / .cpp        <-- Serialization for On-Demand Queries (REP-013) & Snapshots (REP-014)
│   │
│   └── display/
│       └── display_canvas.h / .cpp         <-- Data-driven OLED layout renderer (Zero climate/node knowledge)
│
└── nodes/
    └── eq/                                 <-- EQ-SPECIFIC IMPLEMENTATION
        ├── eq_node.yaml                    <-- ESPHome base: pinouts, I2C buses, Wi-Fi/Ethernet, HAL glue
        ├── eq_om_tables.h / .cpp           <-- Compiled declarative Boolean tables for Fan & Flap interlocks
        ├── controllers/
        │   ├── eq_fan_control.h / .cpp     <-- Target scaling, ramp profiler, calibration state machine, tachless fallback
        │   ├── eq_flap_control.h / .cpp    <-- 2–20pt Fan→Flap curve lookup, proportional modes, close takeover
        │   ├── eq_climate_engine.h / .cpp  <-- Auto equilibrium math, deadbands, response modes, sensor loss fallback
        │   ├── eq_compliance_engine.h / .cpp<-- Thermostat purge handshake, T3 run timer, Hub-loss abort
        │   └── eq_mode_manager.h / .cpp    <-- Whole-device state machine (AUTO, MANUAL, COMPLIANCE, OFF)
        │
        └── eq_app.h / .cpp                 <-- Top-level coordinator gluing ESPHome loops to the engines
```

# Part 2: Step-by-Step Implementation Pipeline

---

## Phase 1: Foundation, Types & Clock Engine (The Base)
*Objective: Build the zero-dependency primitive layer required by every other file.*

```text
FILES TO CREATE:
└── global/core/garuda_types.h
└── global/core/monotonic_clock.h / .cpp
```

### 1. `global/core/garuda_types.h`
* **Contents & Responsibilities:**
  * **Resource Quality Enum:** `QUALITY_GOOD`, `QUALITY_STALE`, `QUALITY_INVALID`, `QUALITY_TIMEOUT`, `QUALITY_DISCONNECTED`, `QUALITY_UNAVAILABLE`, `QUALITY_UNKNOWN`.
  * **Operational Matrix Outcomes (`GLOB-39`):** `OM_PERMITTED = 0`, `OM_DEGRADED = 1`, `OM_UNAVAILABLE = 2`, `OM_PROHIBITED = 3`.
  * **Command Outcomes:** `CMD_REJECTED`, `CMD_BLOCKED`, `CMD_PENDING`, `CMD_APPLIED`, `CMD_FAILED`, `CMD_TIMED_OUT`.
  * **Canonical Unit Definitions:** Base numeric types for floating-point Kelvin (`kelvin_t`), Pressure (`pascals_t`), Volume (`liters_t`), and Logical Percentages (`percent_t` from `0.0f` to `100.0f`).
  * **Reason Codes (`GLOB-25`):** Standard machine-readable numeric constants for rejections, faults, and states.

### 2. `global/core/monotonic_clock.h / .cpp`
* **Contents & Responsibilities:**
  * Uses ESP32 `esp_timer_get_time()` to track continuous, non-decreasing 64-bit microsecond uptime (`monotonic_time_t`).
  * **Guarantees:** Immune to network NTP corrections, clock drift adjustments, or DST stepping (`SCH-01`).
  * **Classes/Utilities:**
    * `ElapsedTimer`: In-memory helper to check if non-blocking intervals (e.g., settling times, debounce windows) have expired.
    * `DeadlineTimer`: Sets a hard point in monotonic time that triggers a timeout condition.
* **Phase 1 Verification Milestone:** A standalone C++ test validating that `garuda_types.h` compiles with zero external dependencies and that `ElapsedTimer` tracks milliseconds deterministically without being affected by system clock changes.

---

## Phase 2: Pure Hardware Actuator Drivers (The Physical Boundary)
*Objective: Build the low-level physical drivers. They own raw GPIOs, PWM timers, interrupts, and settling mechanics, with zero knowledge of climate or control policy.*

```text
FILES TO CREATE:
└── global/drivers/fan_driver.h / .cpp
└── global/drivers/servo_driver.h / .cpp
```

### 1. `global/drivers/fan_driver.h / .cpp`
* **Contents & Responsibilities:**
  * **Hardware Ownership:** Owns ESP32 LEDC PWM channel configuration, Relay GPIO toggling, hardware pulse-counting ISR (tachometer), and ADC sampling (current sense).
  * **Execution Mechanics (`GLOB-11`, `FAN-01..05`):**
    * Maps logical $0\text{--}100\%$ input across configured `pwm_min` and `pwm_max` duty cycles.
    * Executes the staged startup sequence: Relay ON $\to 500\text{ ms}$ settle $\to 2.0\text{s}$ low-speed boost ($20\%$ PWM) $\to$ normal ramp.
    * Executes the staged shutdown sequence: PWM to $0\% \to$ confirm stop $\to 10\text{s}$ dissipation delay $\to$ Relay OFF $\to$ start restart lockout timer.
    * Filters tachometer pulse interrupts into a clean RPM float.
    * Implements raw emergency detach: de-energizes the relay immediately and drops PWM to 0.
  * **Interface Exposed:**
    ```cpp
    void init(const FanDriverConfig &cfg);
    void set_target_pwm_pct(percent_t logical_target);
    void emergency_stop();
    float get_measured_rpm() const;
    float get_measured_current_amps() const;
    bool is_relay_energized() const;
    ```

### 2. `global/drivers/servo_driver.h / .cpp`
* **Contents & Responsibilities:**
  * **Hardware Ownership:** Owns the LEDC servo PWM pulse generator ($50\text{ Hz}$, $1.0\text{--}2.0\text{ ms}$ pulses).
  * **Execution Mechanics (`FLP-01..04`):**
    * Maps logical $0\text{--}100\%$ into configured physical microsecond bounds (`min_us`, `max_us`), with optional directional inversion.
    * Slew rate stepping engine: steps in $1\%$ increments every $50\text{ ms}$ to prevent gear stripping.
    * Settling & coil detach: holds target for $5.0\text{ seconds}$ in `SETTLING` state, then detaches PWM output to prevent hum and coil burnout.
    * Implements emergency detach: unpowers the servo coils immediately at the current physical position without commanding open or close motion.
  * **Interface Exposed:**
    ```cpp
    void init(const ServoDriverConfig &cfg);
    void step_process(); // Called on periodic FreeRTOS tick
    void command_position_pct(percent_t target_position);
    void emergency_detach();
    FlapMovementState get_movement_state() const; // CLOSED, OPENING, OPEN, CLOSING, SETTLING
    percent_t get_current_commanded_position() const;
    ```
* **Phase 2 Verification Milestone:** Flash onto the ESP32. Verify with an oscilloscope/logic analyzer that the Fan Driver executes the $500\text{ ms}$ relay delay and $2\text{s}$ boost, and that the Servo Driver steps at $50\text{ ms}$ intervals and detaches PWM precisely $5\text{ seconds}$ after stopping.

---

## Phase 3: The Data & Decision Engines (Inputs & Safety Gating)
*Objective: Build the sensor ingestion pipeline and declarative safety evaluation engine.*

```text
FILES TO CREATE:
└── global/core/resource_engine.h / .cpp
└── global/core/operational_matrix.h
└── nodes/eq/eq_om_tables.h / .cpp
```

### 1. `global/core/resource_engine.h / .cpp`
* **Contents & Responsibilities:**
  * **Logical Resource Registry (`GLOB-32`):** Central catalog storing logical inputs (e.g., `RES_INTERNAL_TEMP`, `RES_EXTERNAL_HUMIDITY`).
  * **Provider Ingestion & Quality Tagging (`GLOB-43`, `GLOB-44`):** Providers push raw values. Engine tags them with `source_timestamp` (measurement time) and `receive_timestamp` (local receipt time).
  * **Canonical Normalization (`GLOB-46`):** Converts Celsius/Fahrenheit to Kelvin automatically upon ingestion.
  * **Provider Arbitration & Stability Window (`GLOB-33`):**
    * Filters out ineligible providers (corrupted checksum, disconnected, out of range).
    * Evaluates eligible candidates: default is freshness-first, with priority as a tie-breaker.
    * Enforces a configurable stability window before switching active authority to prevent provider flapping.
  * **Stale & Expired Aging Engine (`GLOB-42`):** Runs periodically; marks values `STALE` after the freshness window, and `EXPIRED` (unusable) after the retention limit. Invalid packets do not refresh data age.
  * **Interface Exposed:**
    ```cpp
    void register_resource(ResourceId id, ResourceType type);
    void ingest_provider_data(ResourceId id, ProviderId provider, float val, UnitType unit, QualityTag quality, monotonic_time_t src_time);
    ResourceReading get_resource(ResourceId id) const; // Returns value in Kelvin/SI + QualityTag
    ```

### 2. `global/core/operational_matrix.h`
* **Contents & Responsibilities:**
  * **Declarative AND-Engine (`GLOB-36..39`):** A zero-allocation C++ template that evaluates an array of Boolean condition predicates using conjunctive AND semantics.
  * **Severity Precedence Resolver:** Resolves conflicting condition flags to the single most restrictive outcome (`PERMITTED < DEGRADED < UNAVAILABLE < PROHIBITED`).
  * **Interface Exposed:**
    ```cpp
    struct OmPredicate {
        bool condition_met;
        OmOutcome failure_outcome;
        ReasonCode failure_reason;
    };
    OmResult evaluate_matrix(const OmPredicate* predicates, size_t count);
    ```

### 3. `nodes/eq/eq_om_tables.h / .cpp`
* **Contents & Responsibilities:**
  * Contains the compiled EQ-specific safety tables for Fan and Flap operations.
  * **Fan Matrix Predicates:**
    * Emergency Stop Input is Inactive (fails to `PROHIBITED`).
    * Maintenance Lockout is Inactive (fails to `PROHIBITED`).
    * Flap satisfies required open position OR Flap is disabled (fails to `PROHIBITED`).
    * Primary temperature sensors are valid/fresh (fails to `DEGRADED`).
  * **Flap Matrix Predicates:**
    * Emergency Stop Input is Inactive (fails to `PROHIBITED`).
    * If Fan is running, target must be $\ge$ minimum curve opening OR be exact 0% manual takeover (fails to `PROHIBITED`).
* **Phase 3 Verification Milestone:** Ingest simulated temperature samples into the Resource Engine and confirm automatic conversion to Kelvin and proper `STALE` aging. Pass true/false arrays into `eq_om_tables` and verify that any false safety predicate immediately evaluates to `OM_PROHIBITED`.

---

## Phase 4: Diagnostics, Faults & Non-Volatile Memory
*Objective: Build fault classification, NVS memory management, and power-on safety reconciliation.*

```text
FILES TO CREATE:
└── global/core/fault_engine.h / .cpp
└── global/core/persistence_manager.h / .cpp
```

### 1. `global/core/fault_engine.h / .cpp`
* **Contents & Responsibilities:**
  * **Three-Dimension State Tracking (`GLOB-26`):**
    * *Dimension 1: Observed Health* (raw live sensor state).
    * *Dimension 2: Active Fault* (debounced failure meeting thresholds).
    * *Dimension 3: Retained Latch* (persisted safety lockout in NVS).
  * **Triple-Window Debounce Filter (`FAN-03`):** Increments on consecutive bad evaluations; trips active fault on count $\ge 3$. Resets on clean evaluations.
  * **POST Reconciliation Engine (`FLT-02`):** Runs at boot. Healthy POST clears transient faults, but **leaves retained latches active**.
  * **Clear-Fault Override Handler (`FLT-03`):** Validates manual clear commands; resets latches in NVS to allow the OM to re-evaluate conditions fresh.
  * **Interface Exposed:**
    ```cpp
    void report_observation(FaultId id, bool is_healthy);
    bool is_fault_active(FaultId id) const;
    bool is_latched(FaultId id) const;
    void execute_clear_fault(FaultId id);
    void reconcile_post_boot(const PostResults &post);
    ```

### 2. `global/core/persistence_manager.h / .cpp`
* **Contents & Responsibilities:**
  * **ESP32 NVS Wrapper (`GLOB-14`, `NVS-01`):** Manages atomic flash reads and writes with zero runtime allocation.
  * **Integrity Engine (`NVS-02`):** Wraps data blocks in CRC32 checksums and schema version headers. On CRC mismatch, discards the block and falls back to verified factory defaults with an error log.
  * **Debounce Coalescing Buffer:** Caches incoming configuration changes in RAM; commits to flash only after input settles (e.g., 3.0 seconds after the last slider adjustment).
  * **Explicit Reset Scope Execution (`NVS-03`):** Independent methods for `reboot()`, `reset_config_defaults()`, `reset_calibration_defaults()`, and `factory_wipe()`.
  * **Interface Exposed:**
    ```cpp
    bool load_config_block(ConfigBlockId id, void* dest, size_t len);
    void stage_config_write(ConfigBlockId id, const void* src, size_t len);
    void process_persistence_tick(); // Checks debounce timers & commits to NVS
    ```
* **Phase 4 Verification Milestone:** Force a power-cycle during a debounce window to verify flash integrity. Verify that a simulated overcurrent latch survives a reboot and cannot be cleared until physical sensor health reports clean.

---

## Phase 5: EQ Subsystem Actuator Controllers
*Objective: Build the intelligence layer for the Fan and Flap. These components translate control targets into safe driver commands using the Operational Matrix.*

```text
FILES TO CREATE:
└── nodes/eq/controllers/eq_fan_control.h / .cpp
└── nodes/eq/controllers/eq_flap_control.h / .cpp
```

### 1. `nodes/eq/controllers/eq_fan_control.h / .cpp`
* **Contents & Responsibilities:**
  * **Policy Owner for the Fan Domain:** Consumes logical targets ($0\text{--}100\%$) from climate engines or manual inputs.
  * **Operational Matrix Integration:** Checks the Fan OM prior to every target change. If the OM returns `PROHIBITED`, forces effective target to $0\%$.
  * **Slew-Rate Ramp Profiler:** Smoothly ramps output percentage toward the active target at the configured slew rate ($\%/\text{sec}$).
  * **Feedback Evaluation Loop:** Runs every control tick. Evaluates tachometer RPM and current sense against the triple-window filter in the Fault Engine.
  * **6-State Calibration State Machine (`FAN-05`):** Orchestrates the deterministic calibration steps ($0, N, \dots, 100\%$), managing the 3-attempt stabilization window per point and atomic commit to flash.
  * **Tachless Fallback Policy:** Switches to time-based open-loop limits when tachometer feedback is unavailable.

### 2. `nodes/eq/controllers/eq_flap_control.h / .cpp`
* **Contents & Responsibilities:**
  * **Policy Owner for the Flap Domain:** Evaluates active flap target based on the selected mode:
    * *`PROPORTIONAL_CURVE`:* Interpolates the active Fan target across the 2–20 point monotonic curve.
    * *`FULL_OPEN_FULL_CLOSED`:* Binary mapping ($0\%$ fan = minimum flap opening; $>0\%$ fan = $100\%$ open).
  * **Manual Close Takeover Engine (`FFI-03`):**
    * Intercepts manual $0\%$ flap commands while the fan is spinning.
    * Saves active Fan mode to volatile RAM.
    * Forces Fan domain to $0\%$ and monitors spin-down.
    * Once the fan confirms physical stop, commands the flap closed.
    * Restores the saved operating mode if manual takeover releases.
  * **Flap POST Sweeper:** Commands logical min $\to$ max $\to$ min at boot to verify driver step completion.
* **Phase 5 Verification Milestone:** Connect physical Fan and Servo to the ESP32. Command Fan to $80\%$; verify the Flap opens first before the Fan starts. Command Flap to $0\%$ while running; verify the Fan safely ramps to $0$ and halts before the Flap closes.

---

## Phase 6: The Autonomous Climate Brain
*Objective: Implement the core differential climate balance logic and system mode state machine.*

```text
FILES TO CREATE:
└── nodes/eq/controllers/eq_climate_engine.h / .cpp
└── nodes/eq/controllers/eq_mode_manager.h / .cpp
```

### 1. `nodes/eq/controllers/eq_climate_engine.h / .cpp`
* **Contents & Responsibilities:**
  * **Environmental Equilibrium Calculation (`AUT-01`):**
    * Consumes indoor and outdoor temperature/humidity from the Resource Engine.
    * Evaluates directional utility:
      * *Cooling:* Requires $\text{Outdoor} < \text{Indoor}$. Destination clamped to $\max(\text{Low Setpoint}, \text{Outdoor})$.
      * *Heating:* Requires $\text{Outdoor} > \text{Indoor}$. Destination clamped to $\min(\text{High Setpoint}, \text{Outdoor})$.
    * If outdoor air cannot assist, forces Fan Target to $0\%$.
  * **Proportional Target Math (`AUT-02`):**
    $$\text{Target} = (\text{Indoor Error in Kelvin}) \times \text{Response Ratio}$$
    Response ratio selectable via mode: `QUIET`, `PERFORMANCE`, or `MANUAL`.
  * **Deadbands & Floor Scaling (`AUT-03`):**
    * Applies $0.5\text{ K}$ temperature and $3.0\%\text{ RH}$ deadbands.
    * Enforces product-life floor: $<5.0\% = 0\%$, $5.0\text{--}10.0\% = 10.0\%$, $\ge 10.0\% = \text{Target}$.
  * **Degraded Sensor Fallback Engine (`AUT-04`):**
    * Monitors primary sensor quality. On `UNAVAILABLE` or `EXPIRED`, holds last valid target and starts a 5-minute monotonic countdown.
    * If the timer expires, transitions system to `MANUAL` mode at the last valid speed and logs an alarm.

### 2. `nodes/eq/controllers/eq_mode_manager.h / .cpp`
* **Contents & Responsibilities:**
  * **Whole-Device State Machine (`MCA-01..03`):** Manages `OFF`, `AUTO/TEMPERATURE`, `AUTO/HUMIDITY`, `MANUAL`, and `COMPLIANCE`.
  * **Implicit Submode Transitions:** Automatically transitions to `AUTO/TEMPERATURE` when temperature setpoints change, and `AUTO/HUMIDITY` when humidity setpoints change.
  * **Domain Authority Arbiter:** Resolves conflicts between Auto climate targets, manual user requests, and safety constraints.
* **Phase 6 Verification Milestone:** Feed simulated indoor/outdoor temperatures into the node. Confirm that when outdoor air is hotter than indoor air, cooling output remains at $0\%$. Verify that targets between $1\%$ and $4\%$ snap to zero, targets between $5\%$ and $9\%$ snap to $10\%$, and valid targets scale proportionally.

---

## Phase 7: Inter-Node Compliance, Command Bus & Serialization
*Objective: Build the Thermostat coordination handshake, the unified command dispatcher, and data serialization.*

```text
FILES TO CREATE:
└── global/core/command_manager.h / .cpp
└── nodes/eq/controllers/eq_compliance_engine.h / .cpp
└── global/comms/comms_contracts.h / .cpp
```

### 1. `global/core/command_manager.h / .cpp`
* **Contents & Responsibilities:**
  * **Canonical Command Gateway (`GLOB-10`):** Single point of entry for all incoming commands (REST, BLE, Modbus, Physical Rotary Dial).
  * **Parameter & Authority Gate:** Validates ranges and verifies sender permissions before dispatching.
  * **Outcome Lifecycle Tracking:** Tracks commands through `REJECTED`, `BLOCKED`, `PENDING`, `APPLIED`, `FAILED`, and `TIMED_OUT`.
  * **Post-Commit Event Dispatcher (`CEP-01`):** Emits notifications over the local event bus only after state machines commit changes.

### 2. `nodes/eq/controllers/eq_compliance_engine.h / .cpp`
* **Contents & Responsibilities:**
  * **Thermostat Handshake Protocol Handler (`CMP-01..04`):**
    * Ingests `COMPLIANCE_REQUEST (HEAT | COOL)`.
    * Checks local OM, hardware readiness, and thermal delta. Returns `ACCEPTED`, `NOT_USEFUL`, or `RESOURCE_UNAVAILABLE`.
  * **Purge Cycle Controller:** Runs ventilation targeting external convergence within the $1.0\text{ K}$ band.
  * **T3 Dynamic Runtime Timer:** Starts the maximum duration timer ($5\text{ minutes}$) only after the fan physically moves air (T3).
  * **Hub-Loss Safety Watchdog:** Terminates active compliance purge if Hub communication drops, restoring autonomous local operation.

### 3. `global/comms/comms_contracts.h / .cpp`
* **Contents & Responsibilities:**
  * **On-Demand Sensor Query Handler (`REP-013`):** Serializes individual sensor readings, quality tags, and timestamps into JSON/binary payloads for requesting peers.
  * **Full Node State Snapshot Serializer (`REP-014`):** Packages complete device state (identity, modes, resources, actuator positions, active faults, and latches) into a single atomic snapshot for dashboard sync.
* **Phase 7 Verification Milestone:** Simulate a Thermostat compliance request via HTTP. Verify proper handshake return codes (`ACCEPTED` vs `NOT_USEFUL`). Confirm that disconnecting the network cable during an active purge immediately halts the fan and returns the system to local auto control.

## Phase 8: Presentation, ESPHome HAL & Live Inspection (The Finish Line)
*Objective: Wire the core engines into the ESPHome event loop and build the display/inspection interfaces.*

```text
FILES TO CREATE:
```

* **Contents & Responsibilities:**
  * **Reusable Data-Driven OLED Renderer:** Has zero knowledge of climate, fan curves, or tank volumes.
  * **Interface Exposed:** Accepts an array of generic display blocks:
    ```cpp
    struct DisplayLineBlock {
        uint8_t line_index;
        const char* label;
        char value_str[16];
        QualityTag quality;
    };

        percent_t fill_pct;
    };
    ```

  * Writes directly to the SSD1306/SH1106 buffer via ESPHome display drivers.

### 2. `nodes/eq/eq_app.h / .cpp`
* **Contents & Responsibilities:**
  * Subclasses ESPHome's `Component` and `CustomComponent`.
  * Initializes all global and EQ-specific engines during `setup()`.
  * Runs the primary non-blocking loop during `loop()`:
    1. Update monotonic timers.
    2. Read hardware sensors and push raw samples to the Resource Engine.
    3. Evaluate Operational Matrix safety rules.
    4. Run Climate Engine / Mode Manager loops.
    5. Step Actuator Drivers (Fan ramp, Flap servo slew).
    6. Process flash persistence debouncing.
    7. Format telemetry and update the display canvas.

### 3. `nodes/eq/eq_node.yaml`
* **Contents & Responsibilities:**

  * Declares physical GPIO allocations: I2C bus (`SDA`/`SCL`), Fan PWM pin, Fan Relay pin, Tachometer pulse input, Servo PWM pin, and Emergency Stop dry contact.
  * Includes the custom C++ directories (`global/` and `nodes/eq/`).
  * Binds native ESPHome web server endpoints to the read-only `/status` telemetry handler.
* **Phase 8 Verification Milestone:** Compile and flash the full firmware onto the target hardware via ESPHome. Open `/status` in a web browser to verify live telemetry streaming, and observe the OLED display rendering live line and bar blocks smoothly.

# Part 3: Deep Dive: Global Driver vs. Node Controller Pattern

To show how this separation works in code, here is the architectural contract between the **Global Fan Driver** and the **EQ Fan Controller**:

### 1. The Global Fan Driver (`global/drivers/fan_driver.h`)
*Has zero climate knowledge. Cares only about physical hardware execution.*
```cpp
// global/drivers/fan_driver.h
#pragma once
#include "../core/garuda_types.h"
#include "../core/monotonic_clock.h"

struct FanDriverConfig {
    uint8_t pwm_pin;
    uint8_t relay_pin;
    uint8_t tach_pin;
    uint32_t pwm_frequency_hz;
    percent_t physical_pwm_min; // e.g., 30.0%
    percent_t physical_pwm_max; // e.g., 100.0%
};

class FanDriver {
public:
    void init(const FanDriverConfig &cfg);
    
    // Commands physical hardware based on logical 0-100% input
    void set_logical_target(percent_t logical_pct);
    void process_tick(); // Handles ramp slew, boost timing, shutdown delays
    void emergency_stop_immediate(); // Drops relay and PWM instantly
    
    float get_rpm() const;
    bool is_running() const;
    
private:
    FanDriverConfig config_;
    percent_t current_pwm_out_;
    ElapsedTimer boost_timer_;
    ElapsedTimer shutdown_timer_;
    // Direct hardware register & GPIO controls reside here
};
```

### 2. The EQ Fan Controller (`nodes/eq/controllers/eq_fan_control.h`)
*Has zero GPIO knowledge. Cares only about climate policies and safety interlocks.*
```cpp
// nodes/eq/controllers/eq_fan_control.h
#pragma once
#include "../../global/core/garuda_types.h"
#include "../../global/drivers/fan_driver.h"
#include "../eq_om_tables.h"

class EqFanController {
public:
    EqFanController(FanDriver &driver) : driver_(driver) {}
    
    // Ingests climate targets, checks safety, and controls the driver
    void apply_climate_target(percent_t target_pct) {
        // 1. Evaluate safety permissions via the local Operational Matrix
        OmResult result = EqOmTables::evaluate_fan_operation(FAN_OP_RUN);
        
        if (result.outcome == OM_PROHIBITED) {
            driver_.set_logical_target(0.0f); // Safety prohibits operation
            return;
        }
        
        // 2. If permitted, pass the validated target to the hardware driver
        driver_.set_logical_target(target_pct);
    }
    
    void trigger_emergency_halt() {
        driver_.emergency_stop_immediate();
    }

private:
    FanDriver &driver_; // Talks to the driver abstraction, never raw registers
};
```

# SYSTEM DIRECTIVE: Senior Systems Architect & Embedded Firmware Lead (Garuda Controls)

You are acting as the Lead Embedded Systems Architect and Principal Firmware Engineer for the **Garuda Controls** ecosystem (Hub/Master, Equalizer/EQ, Thermostat/HVAC, and Tanks Node), targeting the ESP32 platform with an ESPHome configuration base and custom native C/C++ (`.c`, `.cpp`, `.h`) core engines.

Below this prompt is a comprehensive project dump containing our authoritative architectural standards, node roadmaps, and implementation code across three core files:

1. **Garuda Controls — Global Architectural Constitution & Decision Register** (Ecosystem Tier 1 Authority)
2. **EQ Architecture Roadmap & Stage Record** (EQ Tier 2 Authority)
3. **Implementation Plan / Concrete Source Code Dump** (Tier 3 Concrete Implementation)

---

### GOVERNANCE & SOURCE-OF-TRUTH HIERARCHY

You must strictly enforce the ecosystem governance hierarchy:
text
GLOBAL RULES (Tier 1) → NODE-SPECIFIC RULES (Tier 2) → IMPLEMENTATION / C++ (Tier 3)

- **Rule of Precedence:** Implementation code must strictly adhere to EQ specifications, which in turn must strictly adhere to Global standards. Code may specialize, but never silently violate or weaken a higher-tier architectural rule.
- **No Hallucinations / No Placeholders:** Never invent unapproved behavior, arbitrary numerical constants, or unstated pinouts. Never generate truncated or placeholder code (`// TODO`, `/* implement later */`).
- **Embedded Constraints:** This firmware runs on ESP32 silicon. All control loops and hot paths must be non-blocking, memory-bounded, avoid runtime heap allocations (`malloc`/`new`), and use monotonic time for durations.

---

### YOUR REQUIRED ACTIONS (EXECUTE IN ORDER)

Before generating new implementation files, thoroughly analyze the entire dump and produce a structured technical audit covering the following 7 sections:

#### 1. File Catalog & Inventory

- Parse and catalog every file, module, and document included in the dump.
- State its authority level (Tier 1 Global, Tier 2 Node, Tier 3 Implementation), its exact purpose, and its architectural ownership boundary.

#### 2. Comprehensive Architectural & Code Reading

- Confirm you have read and mapped the full contents of all files.
- Verify cross-file alignment (e.g., ensure that definitions in implementation match `garuda_types.h`, the Operational Matrix matches `GLOB-36..40`, and state machines match the locked specifications).

#### 3. Establish What Has Been Done (Completed Baseline)

- Catalog all architectural decisions, state machines, drivers, engines, and code modules that are fully specified, verified, and complete.
- Highlight which engines (Resource Engine, Fault Engine, Operational Matrix, Command Pipeline, Flash Persistence, etc.) have completed their architecture definition.

#### 4. Establish What Is Yet to Be Done (Outstanding Scope)

- Enumerate exactly what is missing to take the current state to a fully compiling, hardware-verified milestone.

  - Layer bypasses (e.g., UI or controllers touching hardware registers/GPIO directly instead of routing through physical drivers).
  - Cardinal separation violations (Configuration vs. Runtime State vs. Command vs. Event).
  - Clock errors (using wall-clock NTP time for control durations instead of non-decreasing monotonic time).
  - Operational Matrix discrepancies (improper outcome severity resolution, logic errors in conjunctive AND equations).
  - Fault model errors (conflating live sensor health with latched safety restrictions).

#### 6. Remediation Strategy (How to Correct It)

- Provide a concrete, technical correction plan for every defect identified in Step 5.
- Specify the exact design changes, refactorings, or data structure adjustments required to bring the system into 100% compliance with the Global Constitution.

- Define the exact step-by-step execution roadmap to complete the current implementation stage.
- Present the exact order in which files must be written, compiled, and verified.
- Clearly state the single next concrete file or module we must write right now to begin execution.

---

To build the new architecture while keeping the node **deployable and functional at every single step**, we cannot write all the C++ abstractions at once and hope they compile together at the end. Instead, we follow an **iterative vertical slice** approach:

Each stage introduces one physical or logical capability, pairs a piece of the reusable `global/` infrastructure with its `nodes/eq/` implementation, hooks it into the ESPHome YAML configuration, and flashes the ESP32 to verify real silicon behavior before proceeding.

Here is the revised, concrete step-by-step implementation roadmap from bare bones to the fully autonomous Equalizer.

---

### Master Directory Layout Reference

All stages adhere to this final split:

```text
garuda_firmware/
├── global/                           <-- Shared by EQ, Thermostat, Tanks, Hub
│   ├── core/                         <-- Types, Clocks, Resources, OM, Faults, Persistence
│   ├── drivers/                      <-- Raw hardware abstractions (PWM, Relays, Servos)
│   ├── comms/                        <-- Serialization contracts (REP-013, REP-014)
│   └── display/                      <-- Canvas rendering logic
└── nodes/
    └── eq/                           <-- Equalizer specific
        ├── config/                   <-- Defaults, pinouts, NVS schemas
        ├── controllers/              <-- Climate, Fan, Flap, Compliance engines
        ├── eq_om_tables.h/.cpp       <-- Concrete Fan/Flap safety matrices
        ├── eq_app.h/.cpp             <-- Coordinator tying ESPHome loop to engines
        └── equalizer.yaml            <-- Top-level ESPHome manifest
```

## Stage 1: The Core Type System, Monotonic Clock & Health Engine

*Goal: Implement deterministic timing and health states without tying them to sensor logic yet.*

### 1. Architectural Scope & Deployment State

* Replaces legacy `millis()`-based ad-hoc timing and integer masks (`vent_logic_mask`) with a 64-bit microsecond monotonic clock (`GLOB-24`, `SCH-01`).
- Defines system-wide standardized enums for quality, operational matrix results, and diagnostic reason codes.

### 2. Files Created

* `global/core/garuda_types.h` (Full type definitions: `QualityTag`, `OmOutcome`, `CmdOutcome`, `kelvin_t`, `percent_t`, `ReasonCode`).
- `global/core/monotonic_clock.h` & `.cpp` (`ElapsedTimer`, `DeadlineTimer` wrapping `esp_timer_get_time()`).
- `nodes/eq/eq_app.h` & `.cpp` (Subclasses `esphome::Component`, initialized in `equalizer.yaml`).

### 3. Concrete Parameters & Configuration

* **Monotonic Timers:** Resolution in microseconds (`uint64_t`).
- **Reason Codes Defined:** `REASON_OK = 0`, `REASON_INIT = 1`, `REASON_ESTOP = 10`, `REASON_LOCKOUT = 11`, `REASON_STALL = 12`, `REASON_SENSOR_TIMEOUT = 20`.

### 4. Operational Matrix & Invariants

* Introduce the universal severity ordering: $\text{PERMITTED (0)} < \text{DEGRADED (1)} < \text{UNAVAILABLE (2)} < \text{PROHIBITED (3)}$.

### 5. Deployable Health Check

* Node boots and exposes an ESPHome text sensor publishing monotonic uptime.
- Manually altering wall-clock time does not cause jitters in `ElapsedTimer` executions.

## Stage 2: Transactional Flash Persistence Core (NVS & CRC)

*Goal: Provide robust, debounced flash storage so subsequent components can persist setpoints and calibration tables without flash wear.*

### 1. Architectural Scope & Deployment State

- Isolates volatile operational states from durable configuration.

### 2. Files Created

- `nodes/eq/config/eq_config_structs.h` (C struct containing user setpoints, PWM limits, and curve points).

### 3. Concrete Parameters & Configuration

* **Debounce Delay:** `setpoint_debounce_s: 3.0` (debounces rapid slider changes in RAM before committing to flash).
- **Default Values:**
  - `target_temp_kelvin`: `295.15 K` ($22.0^\circ\text{C}$).
  - `target_humidity_pct`: `60.0%`.
  - `pwm_min`: `20.0%`, `pwm_max`: `100.0%`.
  - `fan_delta`: `10.0%/\text{K}`.

### 4. Deployable Health Check

* Flash the node. Change a setpoint via web slider.
- Confirm in serial log that the write is delayed by 3 seconds, written with a valid CRC32, and restored across a hard power cycle. Corrupting the NVS partition intentionally triggers automatic fallback to factory defaults.

## Stage 3: Sensor Ingestion & Universal Resource Engine

*Goal: Ingest physical I2C sensors, normalize them to canonical Kelvin/SI units, and handle freshness/staleness tracking.*

### 1. Architectural Scope & Deployment State

  - `bus_external`: SDA 18, SCL 19, 100 kHz.

  - Freshness Window: $5.0\text{ s}$ (`FRESH`).
  - Stale Window: $5.0\text{--}30.0\text{ s}$ (`STALE`).
  - Expiration Limit: $>30.0\text{ s}$ (`EXPIRED` $\to$ `QUALITY_TIMEOUT`).

### 4. Deployable Health Check

## Stage 4: Flap (Vent) Actuator Domain

*Goal: Bring up physical flap servo movement safely with rate limiting, angular inversion, and automatic coil detaching.*

### 1. Architectural Scope & Deployment State

* Direct control of the servo is isolated to the pure `ServoDriver` (`FLP-01..04`).
- Flap movement avoids humming and coil burnout by cutting PWM holding power when stationary.

### 2. Files Created

* `global/drivers/servo_driver.h` & `.cpp` (Pulse mapping, slew-rate stepping, auto-detach).
- `nodes/eq/controllers/eq_flap_control.h` & `.cpp` (Flap positioning logic and movement states).

### 3. Concrete Parameters & Configuration

* **Hardware Output:** Pin 26, LEDC Channel 0, 50 Hz PWM.
- **Calibration Limits:** Pulse width min: `1000 us` (closed), max: `2000 us` (open), `inversion: false`.
- **Slew Timing:** Step size $1.0\%$, step interval $50\text{ ms}$.
- **Settling Window:** Hold position for $5.0\text{ s}$ after reaching target, then detach PWM coils.

### 4. Operational Matrix (Flap OM)

* Declarative rule: `CanMoveFlap = (EmergencyStop == Inactive) && (MaintenanceLockout == Inactive)`.

### 5. Deployable Health Check

* Command flap from $0\%$ to $100\%$ via web slider.
- Verify on an oscilloscope or servo linkage: servo steps smoothly over ~5 seconds, stays energized for 5 seconds after arrival, and completely drops PWM signal thereafter.
